On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 11 v1.0

# The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts

1\. Purpose  
This paper is an Adjudication Form (an auxiliary form of the inversion-discernment group). For host instances of the event-gating class declared in advance, it adjudicates from mechanism-level observations whether the three duties of Field Concretization and the fact of constitution of a Sedimentation-Field hold on the instance; it gives the criteria, the subdomain on which the criteria are valid in both directions, the bounds of Misjudgment and the reasons for ⊥; and it registers candidate hosts in columns.  
The properties adjudicated are the three duties of *The General Form of Field Concretization* and their components: the Trace-Response-set duty TraceSet (components q, r), the organizational-participation duty Part, the historical-dynamics duty HDyn (components Act, HistCond); the fact of constitution Fld(χ); and, among the stronger field types, the in-boundary self-feedback type. By Theorem 1 and Corollary 1 of the General Form, within its working domain  
　　Fld(χ) ⇔ ∃T \[ TraceSet(χ, T) ∧ Part(χ, T) ∧ HDyn(χ, T) \].  
The central problem is this. The two-way adjudication of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* holds only on F-class hosts and under the listed protocols (its Theorems 6 and 7). When the occurrence of a process is decided by Trace-Responses through threshold gating (the firing of a neuron, the formation of atomic switches in a network and the avulsion of a river bed can serve as illustrations of this mechanism form; whether they belong to the same class as Definitions 1–3 must be proved item by item, see Section 8), some instances of such hosts cannot be faithfully encoded into the F layer under the encoding conditions E1–E4 declared in 3.6 (for the antecedents of the obstruction and the encoding routes not excluded, see 3.6), and outside the F class Chapter 3 licenses only positive determinations and gives no negative determination. This paper therefore builds separate criteria in the R layer and answers, on the event-gating class 𝒦\_g: whether the duties can be adjudicated in both directions, on what subdomain and under what antecedents, and where Misjudgment comes from; and how the signal source r is certified by actual action chains.  
The conclusions of this paper fall into seven groups.  
　1\. Working domain and observation: the definition and closure of 𝒦\_g, of which the frozen SNN is a member (Proposition 1); the declared R-layer instance and Lemmas R1–R3; the encoding obstruction for 𝒦\_g within the class of exact encodings that Chapter 3 requires (Propositions N1, N2, N2′; Section 3).  
　2\. Dynamics criteria: the local correspondence lemmas (Lemmas 3.1–3.4, with three negative tiers); exact adjudication of functional dependence on 𝒟₁ (Theorem D1); the adjudication theorem for Part and HDyn (Theorem D2, relative to the declared domain 𝒟₁); the T\_max reduction (Theorem TM; Sections 4 and 5).  
　3\. Source criteria: under the declared source reading, the participation-type source-certification criterion PS and its correspondence theorem COR (sufficient direction); the sufficient-subdomain theorem SUB; the structure theorem STR for same-beat certification and the treatment of overdetermination; the negative-determination lemma S⁻ (Sections 4 and 5).  
　4\. Relation to upstream: compatibility on the intersection with Chapter 3 (Theorem C4′); the source-fidelity condition for D16 of the Mathematical Form (Theorem D16-SF); the in-boundary self-feedback type (Theorems B1–B3); read-back (Lemma IMP-g, Theorem R7; Section 5).  
　5\. Bounds of Misjudgment (Section 6).  
　6\. The adjudication theorem, the condition ledger, representative instances and breach modes (Section 7); the complete algorithmic-level verdict on Instance P of the frozen SNN.  
　7\. The master ledger of candidate hosts, strength, open items and interfaces (Sections 8–11).  
Range of values of the verdicts and evidence levels:  
　A verdict of 1 requires the listed protocols and one certified witness.  
　A verdict of 0 requires antecedents such as a declared intervention domain, a complete inventory and faithful records (𝔅₀ listed in Theorem D2(ii); when tier (b) is used, DOM\[𝒟₁\] and the pending correspondence hypothesis B\_FD\[𝒟₁\] are added); a 0 that depends on a pending correspondence hypothesis carries that hypothesis with the conclusion, and if the hypothesis is withdrawn the verdict changes to the listed reason code.  
　Everything else gives ⊥, with a reason code. ⊥ is not 0.  
　The verdicts are model-mode verdicts: the object adjudicated is the declared program host (algorithmic level); reading back to a material host further requires the counterparts of implementation fidelity and model faithfulness listed in 5.5 of Section 5.  
Preconditions of adjudication:  
(1) The definitions of the properties are closed. The duties are defined by the master formula of Section 5 and the bearing relation of Section 3 of the General Form; the evidence atoms take the R-layer and F-layer definitions D4–D21 of Section 3 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*; the signal source takes the declared source reading of 4.3 in Section 4.  
(2) The observation set is delimited: mechanism-level observation—read-out of event components, process-by-process knockout, local setting of Trace-Response positions, and removal of declared out-of-boundary signals; see 3.3 of Section 3.  
(3) The forward relation is given by established Forms: the R-layer beat-wise sum recovers the post-state (Lemma R1), together with the execution order of Definition 2.  
Strength position:  
　The adjudication theorem is a two-way adjudication relative to the declared intervention domain 𝒟₁. On the multi-beat intervention domain 𝒟\_m there is only sufficient negative determination, and a single-beat witness can still give a sufficient positive determination (D₁ ≠ D\_m; a 0 on 𝒟₁ does not imply a 0 on 𝒟\_m).  
　The source criterion gives only sufficient certification: participation-type sources in full are not exhausted by the criteria of this paper, and failure to be certified is not read as absence of a source.  
　The verdict on the frozen SNN is an algorithmic-level model verdict and does not imply the qualification of neuromorphic hardware instances or of biological neural tissue.  
Theoretical increment: Chapter 3 has no negative determination outside the F class, and its EF4 requires conditioning relations to take the form of same-beat functional support. This paper replaces EF4 by non-interference on the slot hypergraph (tier (a)) and by exact adjudication of functional dependence on the declared domain (tier (b), carrying B\_FD), and so extends two-way adjudication to hosts in which occurrence is gated by Trace-Responses. It advances the signal source from the encoding rule “the carrier is outside the boundary” to the certification “takes part through an actual action chain, with a proof of source bearing,” and gives the encoding condition under which D16 of the Mathematical Form preserves this source. The proofs are elementary (branch covering, affine induction, strong Kleene conjunction, non-interference induction); no claim of methodological novelty is made.

3\. Declaration of Properties and Observation Set  
3.1  Working Domain: the Event-Gating Class 𝒦\_g  
Definition 1 (members of 𝒦\_g and the state space). A member G is given by the following declarations: a set of entry units {1, …, n\_in}, a set of gating units {1, …, n\_out}, and a set of Structural Positions X ⊆ entry × gating (taken fully connected); an entry alphabet Σ\_in (including the rest letter none), pat: Σ\_in → {0,1}^{n\_in}, pat(none) = 0; external letters σ = (a, I), where I ∈ ℚ^{n\_out} is out-of-boundary injection; parameters λ\_j ∈ \[0,1\], θ\_j ∈ ℚ, v\_reset,j ∈ ℚ, d\_x,i, d\_y,j ∈ \[0,1\], A₊, A₋ ≥ 0, ρ ∈ \[0,1\], baselines b\_ij and valid intervals lo\_ij ≤ b\_ij ≤ hi\_ij; protocol switches π = (plastic, bap), which belong to the declaration, not to the state. The state is o = (w, v, x, y): w ∈ ∏\[lo\_ij, hi\_ij\] (Structural Positions), v ∈ ℚ^{n\_out} (gating variables), x, y nonnegative (eligibility traces). v, x, y are auxiliary positions: they belong to B but are not counted as Structural Positions, and whether they bear Trace-Responses is declared separately by the correspondence hypothesis EF1′ (Section 7). The Trace-Response is z\_ij = w\_ij − b\_ij, and T ⊆ X is declared.  
Definition 2 (beat-wise execution order). Given a pre-beat state o and a letter σ = (a, I), with s = pat(a): (1) read in s; (2) integrate, vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + I\_j (reading the pre-beat w); (3) gate, g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; (4) reset, v′\_j = v\_reset,j if g\_j = 1, and vt\_j otherwise; (5) eligibility traces, x′\_i = d\_x,i x\_i + s\_i, y′\_j = d\_y,j y\_j + g\_j; (6) plasticity components, P⁺\_ij = A₊x′\_i when plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j, P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_j when plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i, P^r\_ij = −ρ(w\_ij − b\_ij) when plastic; (7) clipping, S\_ij = w\_ij + P⁺ + P⁻ + P^r, w′\_ij = min(hi\_ij, max(lo\_ij, S\_ij)); (8) read out g.  
Definition 3 (general shape and closure). The gate g\_j is a step function of (v\_j, {w\_ij : s\_i = 1}, I\_j), and its read set gr\_j = {v\_j} ∪ {w\_ij : s\_i = 1} is determined by the letter alone; reset is a constant truncation; clipping is a position-wise projection and 1-Lipschitz; potentiation and depression depend only on (x′, y′, g, s) and do not read w; relaxation reads only the w at its own position. Closure: K is a total, single-valued, deterministic function; w′ ∈ \[lo, hi\]; x′, y′ ≥ 0. Hence every member has a unique trajectory on every declared history. Multiplicative plasticity, negative entry vectors, lateral inhibition between outputs and stochastic gating are not in 𝒦\_g.  
Proposition 1 (frozen SNN ∈ 𝒦\_g; proved). Let G\_SNN be the member with n\_in = 4, n\_out = 2, X fully connected, entry letters A = (1,1,0,0), B = (0,0,1,1), AB = (1,1,1,1) and none, λ = 3/4, θ = 1, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 1/2, A₊ = 1/8, A₋ = 1/16, ρ = 0 (default), b = 1/4, lo = 0, hi = 2, and I the teaching current. One beat of G\_SNN agrees component by component with step of the frozen program snn\_core.py (Appendix A.1). Proof: line-by-line substitution. Integration reads a copy of the pre-beat w; the eligibility traces are updated before potentiation and depression, so x′ and y′ are used; relaxation reads before it writes within the iteration for the same position, so it uses the pre-beat value; the parameters satisfy the constraints of Definition 1. ∎ Finite verification by program: Appendix A.2 implements Definition 2 independently and compares it with the frozen program beat by beat on the full state and every component; over 23194 beats there are 0 disagreements, including 16609 places where clipping takes effect and 3374 where the gating argument exactly equals the threshold (Appendix B.3).

### 3.2  The Declared R-Layer Instance

Definition 4 (declaration). χ\_SNN = (P, L, B, W): P is the frozen program, L the discrete beats of the algorithmic level, B = {4 entry units, 2 output units, 8 synapses}, and W the declared window; the set of Structural Positions X = the 8 synapses. Outside B are the entry stimulus sequence and the teaching current, which are out-of-domain signals relative to B.  
Definition 5 (individuation of events). Each run, in each beat, of each of the following mechanism processes is one event e; c is its component, and reads is the set of pre-beat nodes on which its component and occurrence depend: LEAK\_j (tgt v\_j, c = (λ − 1)v\_j, every beat); SYN\_ij (tgt v\_j, c = w\_ij, when s\_i = 1); INJ\_j (carrier outside the boundary, tgt v\_j, c = I\_j, when I\_j ≠ 0); RESET\_j (tgt v\_j, c = v\_reset − vt\_j, when g\_j = 1); YIN\_j (tgt y\_j, c = 1, when g\_j = 1); YDEC\_j, XDEC\_i (decay, every beat); XIN\_i (tgt x\_i, c = 1, when s\_i = 1); PLUS\_ij (tgt w\_ij, c = A₊x′\_i, when plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j); MINUS\_ij (tgt w\_ij, c = −A₋y′\_j, when plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i); RELAX\_ij (tgt w\_ij, c = −ρ(w\_ij − b\_ij), when plastic ∧ ρ ≠ 0); CLIP\_ij (tgt w\_ij, c = clip(S\_ij) − S\_ij, when clip(S\_ij) ≠ S\_ij). Apart from INJ, every carrier is inside B. The decomposition follows D4 of the Mathematical Form and the process-identification rule of Section 3 of the General Form. Clipping also has a “joint reading” (all components at the same position in the same beat form one indecomposable joint event, D9 of the Mathematical Form); the treatment of the two readings is given in E-g3 of Section 6.  
Definition 6 (K\_t and witness histories). K\_t = {(o, E, o′) : E = Ev(o, σ\_t), o′(n) = o(n) + Σ\_{e∈E, tgt(e)=n} c\_e}; the witness step is RStep\_t = (t, o\_t, o\_{t+1}, Ω\_t, Π\_t), with Π\_t = ∅ (no out-of-boundary pulse acts directly on a Structural Position).  
Definition 7 (the two graphs). The actual event graph Γ\_act is the graph of D13 of the Mathematical Form whose event nodes are only the events that actually occurred and whose edges are read edges, write edges and persistence edges of Structural Positions; it is the path record of the actual processes. The slot hypergraph Γ\_slot has as event nodes every slot that can occur in each beat given the letter (whether or not it occurs in that beat), and as read edges the read sets of the respective kinds; it is an analysis hypergraph used only in the proofs of covering and non-interference. Γ\_act ⊆ Γ\_slot. Reachability on the hypergraph certifies neither a real path nor HistCond = 1.  
Lemma R1 (closure of K\_t; proved). For all G ∈ 𝒦\_g, o, σ and nodes n: o(n) + Σ\_{tgt(e)=n} c\_e = K(o, σ)(n). Proof: the components of v\_j sum to vt\_j − v\_j + \[g\_j\](v\_reset − vt\_j); those of x\_i to (d − 1)x\_i + s\_i; those of y\_j to (d − 1)y\_j + g\_j; those of w\_ij to P⁺ + P⁻ + P^r + (clip(S) − S) = clip(S) − w\_ij. ∎ Hence what Ev lists is the whole action of that beat (completeness of the event inventory holds by the structure of the model).  
Lemma R2 (sufficiency of read sets; proved). The occurrence and component of every event are functions of the values of the nodes in its read set and of the letter (by inspection of Definition 5 kind by kind). ∎  
Lemma R3 (non-interference; proved). Let S be a set of source nodes and R\_t the set forward-reachable from S on the slot hypergraph. For any intervention that changes only the values of nodes in S: at beat t the values of all nodes not in R\_t are unchanged, and events whose read sets are disjoint from R\_t have unchanged occurrence and component. Proof: induction on t; n(t + 1) is a function of the values of the nodes in its slot dependency set and of the letter; the part on events follows from R2. ∎  
Finite verification by program: witnesses generated independently from the logs of the frozen program are compared with Ev beat by beat: 5190 beats and 165598 event witnesses (8628 of them clipping; both figures are sums of the rows in Section 2 of Appendix B.4), 0 disagreements; 50753 perturbations outside the read sets, 0 violations; for non-interference, 43301 node comparisons and 78738 event comparisons, 0 violations (Appendix B.4).  
3.3  Observation Set, Read-Out and Intervention  
Mode: model mode (the object adjudicated is the declared program host; a setting directly rewrites the state variables that the processes read).  
Read-out: RD—the component of each event is readable (faithful in model mode); KO—the knockout difference clip(S) − clip(S − c\_e).  
Local setting LOC: at a beat u at which k is available in the actual history (D17 of the Mathematical Form: z \> ε and formed earlier), set k ∈ T to a rational value in \[b\_k, hi\_k\] (depth ≥ 0), leave the other nodes unchanged, and run from u by Definition 2. Setting belongs to testing, not to intrusion (Dom3 of 4.2 of Chapter 3).  
Declared intervention domains (declared in advance and not changed during adjudication):  
　Definition D-1 (𝒟₁, single-beat joint LOC): take a beat u at which A\_T(u) := {k ∈ T: k is available at beat u} is nonempty, and set all positions of A\_T(u) simultaneously to σ\_k ∈ \[b\_k, hi\_k\]; taking σ at its actual value means “not set,” so single-position settings and subset settings are both contained in 𝒟₁.  
　Definition D-2 (𝒟\_m, multi-beat): settings at arbitrarily many available position–beat pairs. 𝒟₁ ⊆ 𝒟\_m, but conclusions on 𝒟₁ do not imply conclusions on 𝒟\_m.  
Removal (used for r): set some nonzero components of the declared letter σ\_t of beat t to zero—for the entry, take a letter of the declared alphabet whose pattern is position-wise no greater than the actual pattern; for the teaching current, take each component at its original value or 0 (any subset of components, joint removal included). Removal acts only on the declared out-of-domain signals and changes neither the state, nor the process laws, nor the boundary B.  
External clamp (used for the in-boundary self-feedback type): out-of-boundary components and out-of-boundary carrier processes take their values from the actual run, independently of the setting.

### 3.4  Properties Adjudicated and Atoms

For events e within W:  
　a(e) := \[\|c\_e\| \> ε\] (via RD).  
　FD\_𝒟(e) := \[∃ a setting in 𝒟 that changes the occurrence or the component of e\] (what is compared is the original component c, not the threshold-truncated booked term).  
　Path\_slot(e) := \[reads(e) is reachable in Γ\_slot from {(k, u): k ∈ T, u available, u ≤ t(e)}\]; Path\_act(Q, e) := \[reads(e) is reachable in Γ\_act from the node set Q\].  
　η(e) (the atom of HistCond; κ(e), the atom of Cond, is defined isomorphically and used for events with car = B) takes its value by the tiers of 4.2.  
　HDyn(T) = ⋁{\[tgt(e) ∈ T\] ∧ a(e) ∧ η(e)}, Part(T) = ⋁{κ(e): car(e) = B} (D8 of the Mathematical Form).  
　q(T): every position of T has z \> ε at window end and has a formation record (component-wise threshold crossing of D16 of the Mathematical Form; D17). r(T): 4.3. TraceSet = q ∧ r ∧ Ident ∧ HistEv (Ident is given by the Structural Positions being synapses and by persistence of position; HistEv by full records from the declared initial state). Q\_R(T) = TraceSet ∧ Part ∧ HDyn. Fld(χ) is the read-back of ∃T Q\_R(T) (4.4, 5.5).

### 3.5  Forward Relation

The forward relation of the R layer is Definition 2 together with Lemma R1: each post-beat state equals the pre-beat state plus the sum of the components of the events of that beat, and the event inventory is complete. All duty atoms are evaluated on R-layer witnesses, not through an F encoding (last sentence of 8.2(6) of the Mathematical Form: “wherever the Trace-Responses determine the occurrence, timing or position of an action, evaluation uses R-layer witnesses”).  
3.6  Encoding Obstruction: Why Chapter 3 Cannot Be Applied Directly  
The two-way adjudication of Theorems 6 and 7 of Chapter 3 has SEP (the host is of F class), LOC, BEAT and exactness as antecedents. Consider an encoding E of 𝒦\_g instances as F runs that satisfies:  
　(E1) Depth is the Trace-Response: every Structural Position in T is an F position, with depth s\_k = z\_k, and ε agrees with the declared resolution;  
　(E2) BEAT: one host beat corresponds to one F beat, and components read the pre-beat state;  
　(E3) LOC exclusivity: the host's LOC\_k corresponds to a single-coordinate setting in F that changes only (k, t);  
　(E4) Exactness: on the valid domain of LOC\_k, the post-state of position k given by one F beat is the same as the host's (D20 of the Mathematical Form has no tolerance).  
Everything else is left free: the F position set may contain arbitrary augmented coordinates (whose depths are functions of the host state that do not depend on w\_k), and each beat may have any finite number of constant-, linear- and bilinear-law mechanisms and pulses.  
Proposition N1 (impossibility of event-by-event encoding; proved). Let k ∈ T be available at beat t, and suppose the occurrence of PLUS\_k switches by gate threshold crossing at a point w\* in the interior of the valid domain of w\_k, the component a on occurrence satisfying \|a\| \> ε and not depending on w\_k. Then no encoding satisfies E1–E4 and encodes PLUS\_k as a single F mechanism. Proof: the occurrence of an F mechanism is declared and does not change with LOC; its booked component is c\_m(v)·\[\|c\_m(v)\| \> ε\], where c\_m is a polynomial of degree ≤ 2; exactness requires c\_m = a at infinitely many points of \[w\* − b, hi − b\], so c\_m ≡ a, and then a ≠ 0 is booked on \[0, w\* − b), where PLUS\_k does not occur in the host. ∎  
Lemma Q (proved). Under E2 and E3, with the other coordinates of F fixed, let the one-beat update of position k be Φ(v) = v + Σ\_p a\_p + Σ\_m c\_m(v)·\[\|c\_m(v)\| \> ε\]. Then at any point v₀ the difference between the left and right limits lies in εℤ. Proof: when \|c\_m(v₀)\| ≠ ε, the term is continuous at v₀; when \|c\_m(v₀)\| = ε, the left and right limits each take 0 or ±ε; the sum is finite. ∎ What Lemma Q restricts is the height of discontinuous jumps as a function of the LOC variable; the components and updated values of F may be arbitrary rationals.  
Proposition N2 (proved). Suppose the net increment of the host at position k, as a function of w\_k, has a jump J ∉ εℤ at a point w\* in the interior of the valid domain. Then no F encoding satisfies E1–E4, however the mechanisms are decomposed and the augmented coordinates chosen. Proof: E4 requires the two sides to have the same left and right limits at w\*; by Lemma Q the jump on the F side lies in εℤ. ∎  
Proposition N2′ (necessary error of approximate encoding; proved). Suppose an encoding satisfies E1–E3, and let η be the supremum over \[b\_k, hi\_k\] of its difference from the host's post-state at position k. If the host has a jump J at an interior point, then η ≥ dist(J, εℤ)/2. Proof: at the jump point the difference between the left and right limits of the difference function is nε − J; taking points on both sides gives the bound. ∎  
Readings of the antecedents on the frozen SNN (Appendix B.5): Instance P, beat 26, threshold-crossing point w\* = 173871/524288 ∈ (1/4, 2), PLUS\_00 component 0.125070 \> ε, not depending on w00; jump J ≈ 0.062569754, J/ε ≈ 6.256975 ∉ ℤ; dist(J, εℤ) ≈ 0.2570ε, necessary error η ≥ 0.1285ε (this is only a lower bound; no complete approximate encoding attaining this error is given). Finite verification of Lemma Q: 3000 sets of random mechanisms, 13895 candidate jump points, 0 violations.  
Scope: N1 and N2 hold under the assumptions of E1–E4, fixed other coordinates, finitely many mechanisms and threshold points in the interior of the valid domain; they are evidence of an obstruction within the class of encodings that Chapter 3 requires. E1 and E3 are restrictions of this encoding comparison, not necessary conditions for every application of Chapter 3. They prove neither that every state encoding is impossible nor that every member of 𝒦\_g is unencodable: members whose gates never cross threshold within the valid domain may fall into the domain F can handle (this is exactly the intersection of 5.7); sub-beat encodings (violating BEAT), approximate encodings (violating D20), nonlinear changes of the depth variable (violating E1) and finite setting domains (to which the polynomial argument of N1 and N2 does not apply) are not excluded, and each of them makes the antecedents of “direct application” fail. Hence on these instances of 𝒦\_g the Y₂ verdicts of Chapter 3 cannot be obtained through exact encoding, and criteria must be built separately in the R layer.

4\. Construction of the Criteria

### 4.1  Verdicts and Reason Codes

Verdicts take three values {1, 0, ⊥}; conjunction and disjunction follow the strong Kleene rules of D1 of the Mathematical Form. ⊥ carries a reason code:  
　⊥\_Cov: functional dependence on the declared domain was not fully adjudicated within the node budget (or 𝒟\_m is taken as the declared domain and Lemmas C and C′ do not apply);  
　⊥\_Open: there is a functional-dependence witness, but on the actual event graph there is no path starting from the changed positions (the dependence passes only through branches that did not actually occur);  
　⊥\_Over: a tier-(b) 0 without B\_FD declared;  
　⊥\_Sat: only KO read-out is available and clipping is saturated;  
　⊥\_Ind, ⊥\_Joint: the two readings of clipping individuation disagree; under the joint reading the net change is zero while the members are nonzero (D9 of the Mathematical Form);  
　⊥\_OD: in source certification the gate is overdetermined by in-boundary contributions and the NESS test fails;  
　⊥\_σOpen: the dependence on removal passes only through opening a gate that did not actually open, or there is no actual chain and the dependence is transmitted through non-structural positions;  
　⊥\_σOver: there is an actual action chain but no certification method succeeds;  
　⊥\_N′: issued where r would receive a negative determination but the reading N′ is not declared;  
　No ⊥ of any kind implies 0; ⊥\_Over means that, without B\_FD declared, the object level remains unknown.  
4.2  Dynamics Atoms: the Three Tiers of Act and HistCond  
a(e) is read out by RD; when only KO read-out is available, Lemma 3.1(ii) applies.  
Values of η(e) (κ isomorphically):  
　1: FD\_𝒟(e) = 1 with an implemented witness (u, σ) (set of changed positions Q), and Path\_act(Q × {u}, e) = 1;  
　0 (tier (a)): Path\_slot(e) = 0;  
　0 (tier (b)): Path\_slot(e) = 1 while FD\_𝒟(e) = 0 has been adjudicated over the whole declared domain; the object-level 0 carries B\_FD\[𝒟\], and without B\_FD declared the verdict is ⊥\_Over;  
　⊥\_Open: FD = 1 while Path\_act = 0;  
　⊥\_Cov (tier (c)): everything else, including the case in which finitely many settings produce no response but the whole declared domain has not been adjudicated.  
When the declared domain is 𝒟₁, FD is adjudicated exactly by the procedure of Theorem D1.

### 4.3  Source Criterion

Declared source reading (a declared reading clause). “Under the boundary B declared in advance, when an out-of-domain signal, through an actually occurring route of action, takes part in bringing about an effective change sustained and retained at a Structural Position, that change can be certified as having a signal source. Pre-existing Trace-Responses, auxiliary states and in-boundary mechanisms are allowed to take part jointly; the signal is not required to bypass the mechanisms of the medium and rewrite the structure directly. Mere presence of the signal, temporal correlation or reachability in a graph does not suffice for certification. The actual action chain and the proof of source bearing should be retained. Removal comparison may serve as a method of certification, but ‘necessarily changes after removal’ is not a necessary condition in every source case; overdetermined cases must be handled separately.”  
This clause is the declared ontological reading of the source and is used as a reading antecedent; its ontological content is not proved here. What is proved is the correspondence between the criterion below and this reading (Theorem COR). Under this reading the “two kinds of elements” of Section 3 of the General Form are not mutually exclusive: one and the same change can have the participation of a signal and also that of pre-existing Trace-Responses or in-boundary mechanisms. The division into two kinds is still kept—(r) requires that some change in Hist(T) with a signal source falls on T, while the other elements of T may be brought about by in-field continuation. “Outside the domain” is relative to the declared B: an action can be internal to a larger system and at the same time outside B. Within one adjudication B does not change; changing the boundary gives another adjudication instance.  
Write Mark^A(k) for the effective changes on k that have a signal source (defined by the declared source reading; no exhaustive adjudication of it is given), and Mark^PS(k) ⊆ Mark^A(k) for those certified by the criterion of this paper.  
Definition S-1 (field-entry components). The components ('in', i) of the declared letter of beat t whose entry pattern is 1, and the nonzero teaching-current components ('I', j).  
Definition S-2 (actual action chain Ch^D15). Refine the graph of D13 of the Mathematical Form within the beat by the execution order of Definition 2. The nodes are only the events that actually occurred and the gates γ\_j that actually opened; the edges from states to events are given by functional support (D15 of the Mathematical Form: the coefficient is nonzero, regardless of whether the reading is zero): ('in', i) → XIN\_i, SYN\_ij (SYN\_ij feeds into γ\_j when w\_ij ≠ 0), MINUS\_ij (occurrence), PLUS\_ij (component, via x′\_i); ('I', j) → INJ\_j → γ\_j; the carried v\_j feeds into γ\_j when λ\_j ≠ 0; γ\_j → RESET\_j, YIN\_j, PLUS\_ij (occurrence), MINUS\_ij (via y′\_j); the carried x\_i enters the component of PLUS\_ij when d\_x,i ≠ 0, and the carried y\_j enters the component of MINUS\_ij when d\_y,j ≠ 0; CLIP via its members on the chain. Across beats the chain passes only through auxiliary positions: when v\_j is reset, its carry is the origin set of γ\_j; when it is not reset, its carry is the union of what fed in during the beat, joined with the original carry when λ\_j ≠ 0; x\_i and y\_j keep their original carry when d ≠ 0. The chain does not pass through Structural Position nodes. Ch\_0(e) uses only the field entries of the current beat, and Ch\_aux(e) allows crossing beats; their origin sets are written Orig\_0(e) and Orig\_aux(e).  
Definition S-3 (admissible removal). A same-beat removal is admissible if and only if it satisfies the no-opening condition g′\_j ≤ g\_j (for all j). A removal at an earlier beat u is admissible if and only if, when rerunning from u to t(e), (A) the Structural Positions of every beat after u are clamped to their actual values (Trace-Response clamping), and (B) every beat satisfies the no-opening condition. The no-opening condition is imposed only on gates: within a beat of 𝒦\_g, the gate is the only link whose occurrence is decided by the state and which affects other events; clipping acts only on its own position, and its consequences are passed on through the Structural Position in the next beat, where in cross-beat removal they are already clamped.  
Definition S-4 (Clause 0). An effective change at a Structural Position made by an event whose carrier is outside the boundary is directly a change left by the action of an out-of-domain signal. The only event in 𝒦\_g with an out-of-boundary carrier, INJ, acts on v, not on a Structural Position, so Clause 0 is empty in 𝒦\_g.  
Criterion PS (participation-type source certification). Let the event e of beat t act on the Structural Position k. Then e ∈ Mark^PS(k) if  
　PS1  \|c\_e\| \> ε (an effective change sustained and retained at the Structural Position);  
　PS2  on Ch^D15 there is an actual action chain that starts from an out-of-domain signal component and reaches e;  
　PS3  there is a proof of source bearing, by one of the following methods:  
　　M1₀  some admissible same-beat removal changes the occurrence or component of e;  
　　M1\_aux  some admissible removal at an earlier beat changes e (EF1′ is further required);  
　　M2  the NESS test succeeds when the gate is overdetermined (B\_OD is further required): the signal dependence of e passes through the gate γ\_j; the actual components of vt\_j are the in-boundary component λ\_j v\_j and the signal components, and N is the sum of the negative components; there exist a positive signal component c and a subset A of the other positive components such that Σ A + N \< θ\_j ≤ Σ A + N + c.  
B\_OD (overdetermination bearing reading; a pending correspondence hypothesis): when a gate is overdetermined by in-boundary contributions, if the actual positive contribution of a signal is a necessary member of an actually sufficient subset (one that crosses the threshold in the presence of all negative contributions), then the signal takes part in bringing about the writes that occur through this gate. The declared source reading gives no certification criterion for overdetermined cases; whether to adopt B\_OD is listed as open (U1 of Section 10).  
Three-valued rule for r\_R. Among the effective events acting on T: if there is one with M1₀, the verdict is 1; otherwise, if there is one with M1\_aux, it is “1, carrying EF1′”; otherwise, if there is one with M2, it is “1, carrying B\_OD”; otherwise, if all are “no chain” or “continuation,” it is “0, carrying N′ and EF1′” (Lemma S⁻, with antecedents REC, ENT, R1, EF1′ and the reading N′; without N′ declared, ⊥\_N′); otherwise it is ⊥, with the reason codes that occur (⊥\_OD, ⊥\_σOpen, ⊥\_σOver). Clause 0 gives 1 (empty in 𝒦\_g). “Continuation” means that removing an earlier field entry changes e, while on the slot hypergraph with the Structural Position nodes deleted e is not reachable from the removed components: the dependence passes only through Trace-Responses already left (as when relaxation reads the Trace-Response at its own position).  
Reading N′ (a pending correspondence hypothesis, used only for negative determination): the route of action of an out-of-domain signal ends at the Structural Position where it leaves a change; succeeding actions that read the Trace-Responses already left belong to in-field continuation and are not routes of action of that signal. Under the declared source reading, having no actual route of action already means having no signal source, so what remains of N′ is the single item “the route does not pass through Structural Positions.” Another reading, Ψ (the route may continue along Trace-Responses already left, and removal comparison certifies that a prior signal is a source of a succeeding change), is likewise undecided; the two give different verdicts on the separating example of 5.8, and the choice between them is listed as open (U2 of Section 10). N′ enters no positive determination and no fidelity conclusion on D16.

### 4.4  Q\_R, Fld and T\_max

Q\_R(T) = TraceSet(T) ∧ Part(T) ∧ HDyn(T). The Fld verdict is 1 when there exists T with Q\_R(T) = 1 (existence of a witness suffices; 4.7 of Chapter 3); with T\_max := {k ∈ X: z\_k \> ε at window end and there is a formation record} (4.7 of Chapter 3), it is 0 when Q\_R(T\_max) = 0 (which further requires EF1′ and the antecedents consumed by the conjuncts that are 0, Theorem R7(2)); otherwise it is ⊥. Q\_R(T) = 0 holds only for the listed T and does not imply Fld = 0.  
4.5  Verdict on the In-Boundary Self-Feedback Type  
The candidate process domain is 𝒞\_B = {events e within W: car(e) = B, tgt(e) ∈ T}; the atom is ι(e) = (a(e), η^cl(e)), where η^cl is evaluated by the three tiers of 4.2 with external-clamp settings as the interventions, and a positive determination further requires the witness path on the actual event graph to lie entirely within B (otherwise ⊥\_ext); the verdict is ⋁\_{e∈𝒞\_B} a ∧ η^cl, and a tier-(b) 0 carries B\_FD. Closed-loop instances are handled separately by Theorem B3.

5\. Proofs of Validity of the Criteria

### 5.1  Exact Rational Linear Feasibility

Constraints have the form Σ α\_v v + γ ⊲ 0, ⊲ ∈ {≤, \<}, with rational coefficients. Procedure (Appendix A.5): Fourier–Motzkin elimination, with strictness propagated by the rule “if either of the two rows is strict, the derived row is strict,” every derived row carrying nonnegative multipliers with respect to the original constraints. Once all variables are eliminated, a constant row with γ \> 0, or with γ = 0 and strict, gives the verdict infeasible, and its multipliers are output; otherwise a rational witness point is found by back-substitution in reverse order.  
Theorem L1 (correctness; proved). The system is feasible over ℚ if and only if the procedure reports feasible; when it reports feasible, the back-substituted point satisfies all constraints. Proof: with the other variables x′ fixed, x must lie between the greatest lower bound and the least upper bound; this interval is nonempty if and only if every pair of an upper-bound row and a lower-bound row satisfies the corresponding (strict or non-strict) inequality, that is, the derived row; induction on the number of eliminations; back-substitution uses only rational operations. ∎  
Lemma L2 (certificate; proved). When the verdict is infeasible, the multipliers λ ≥ 0 satisfy Σλ\_iα\_i ≡ 0 and Σλ\_iγ\_i \> 0, or Σλ\_iγ\_i = 0 with a positive multiplier on some strict row; this proves infeasibility independently (the easy direction of the Motzkin alternative). ∎  
Lemma L3 (equivalence under simplification; proved). Keeping only the tightest row for each coefficient direction leaves the feasible set unchanged. ∎  
Finite verification: 4000 random systems (dimension 1–4); for the 2210 feasible ones every witness point satisfies every constraint on substitution, and for the 1790 infeasible ones every certificate checks independently; 0 failures (Appendix B.7). Hence every “feasible” in the adjudication comes with a point and every “infeasible” with a certificate, and the conclusions do not depend on trust in the program.

### 5.2  Local Correspondence Lemmas

Lemma 3.1 (Act in both directions; proved). (i) With RD, a(e) = \[\|c\_e\| \> ε\] is word for word the General Form's “a process acts if and only if its component is nonzero and effective”; two-way. (ii) With only KO read-out: if S and S − c\_e both lie in \[lo, hi\], the KO difference = c\_e, two-way; otherwise \|KO difference\| ≤ \|c\_e\|, \|KO difference\| \> ε gives 1, and everything else gives ⊥\_Sat. (iii) The a of CLIP depends on the individuation reading (E-g3 of Section 6). ∎  
Lemma 3.2 (positive certification; proved). Suppose FD\_𝒟(e) = 1 with an implemented witness (u, σ) and set of changed positions Q, and Path\_act(Q × {u}, e) = 1. Then in model mode: (i) the occurrence or strength of e is a non-constant function of the values of the Trace-Responses already left at the positions of Q; (ii) Γ\_act records an actual computational route from these Trace-Responses already left to e. Together these are the functional support and evidence path required by 8.2(5) of the Mathematical Form, so e satisfies (h) of the General Form. Proof: (i) follows from the exclusivity of the setting and the concrete rerun of the witness; (ii) from the definition of Γ\_act. The path and the dependence both start from the same set of changed positions. A witness with u \< t(e) is transmitted across beats through auxiliary nodes, which Section 3 of the General Form expressly permits (“not restricted to direct reading at the same moment”). ∎ If FD = 1 while Path\_act = 0, the verdict is ⊥\_Open.  
Lemma 3.3 (negative tier (a); proved). If Path\_slot(e) = 0, then e is unchanged under every setting in 𝒟\_m, and in Γ\_act e is unreachable from every available node of T; hence, under every reading that requires “through an actual material route,” HistCond(e) = 0 at the object level. Proof: the first half follows from Lemma R3 (taking all available nodes of T as the source set), the second half from Γ\_act ⊆ Γ\_slot. Antecedents: closure of the model and of the inventory. ∎  
Lemma 3.4 (negative tier (b); proved at the criterion level, a pending correspondence hypothesis at the object level). If Path\_slot(e) = 1 while FD\_𝒟(e) = 0, the criterion issues FD\_𝒟(e) = 0; the object-level η(e) = 0 further requires  
　B\_FD\[𝒟\]: on the declared instance, HistCond(e) holds only if some variation within 𝒟 of the Trace-Response values of T changes the occurrence or strength of e.  
B\_FD is not proved by the General Form. Its counterpart in the F layer is D18 of the Mathematical Form, “a path label cannot substitute for functional support,” and under F laws single-beat, single-position functional dependence is exactly the functional support of D15 (Lemma D: for an F mechanism m and an available position i, there is a legitimate δ that changes c\_m if and only if i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s)). Without B\_FD declared, the verdict is ⊥\_Over. This tier is not read as “HDyn = 0 in every overdetermined case.” ∎  
Tier (c): FD has not been fully adjudicated on the declared domain, and the verdict is ⊥\_Cov; a lack of response to finitely many settings places an event in neither tier (a) nor tier (b).

### 5.3  Adjudication Theorems

Theorem D1 (exact adjudication of functional dependence on 𝒟₁; proved). For a member of 𝒦\_g, a declared instance in model mode and an event e within W, the following procedure terminates in finitely many steps and outputs the truth value of FD\_𝒟₁(e), giving a rational witness when the verdict is 1. Procedure (Appendix A.6): for each u ≤ t(e) (with A\_T(u) ≠ ∅), replace w\_k(u) by rational variables σ\_k, with initial region R₀ = ∏\[b\_k, hi\_k\]; from beat u, execute Definition 2 symbolically beat by beat. The gating argument vt\_j is an affine expression in σ and splits into two branches, vt\_j ≥ θ\_j and \< θ\_j; the pre-clip sum of each T position splits into three branches, S \< lo, lo ≤ S ≤ hi and S \> hi; on each branch feasibility is decided by the procedure of 5.1, and infeasible branches are pruned. On each feasible branch the occurrence of e and its affine component c(σ) are computed; if e does not occur on the branch, or one of region ∧ {c \< c₀} and region ∧ {c \> c₀} is feasible, the verdict is 1 and a witness point is extracted.  
Proof: (1) The two gating branches and the three clipping branches of each beat are partitions of the real line, so for each σ in R₀ exactly one branch path contains σ, and its gating and clipping values are those of the actual run under σ. (2) Affinity within a branch, by induction on the number of beats: given the gating values, v after reset is a constant or vt; x′ depends only on the letter; y′ is a constant; the potentiation and depression components are constants; relaxation is affine; given the clipping segment, w′ is lo, hi or S; positions outside T stay constant. (3) By (1) and (2), “there exists σ that changes e” holds if and only if some leaf-branch region is nonempty and on it either e does not occur or c(σ) ≠ c₀ has a solution; the latter is the feasibility of one of two strict linear systems; by Theorem L1 the adjudication is correct, and the witness point is rational and is further checked by a concrete rerun. (4) The depth is finite and the number of branches per beat is ≤ 2^{n\_out}·3^{\|T\|}, so the procedure terminates. (5) 𝒟₁ is the union over the u. ∎  
The complexity is exponential in the window length in the worst case; the program sets a node budget, and events affected by exceeding it receive ⊥\_Cov; no 0 is issued for them.  
Theorem D2 (adjudication theorem for Part and HDyn, relative to 𝒟₁; proved). Take a, η and κ event by event by 4.2 (𝒟 = 𝒟₁), compose HDyn and Part by 3.4, and for clipping individuation take the value on which the two readings agree, otherwise ⊥\_Ind.  
(i) Validity of 1: HDyn = 1 ⇒ there is an event e within W with tgt ∈ T such that Act(e) is 1 (Lemma 3.1) and (h) of the General Form is satisfied (Lemma 3.2); likewise for Part = 1 (Cond).  
(ii) Validity of 0: HDyn = 0 ⇒ every event within W with tgt ∈ T has a = 0, or is in tier (a), or is a tier-(b) 0. Under the antecedents, HDyn(χ, T) = 0 at the object level:  
　𝔅₀ (always): MOD-R (all events within W that act on T are listed, Lemma R1); R2/R3 (closure of the read sets and of the slot graph); REC (the actual history is fully recorded from the declared initial state); ENT (the formative history is recorded from the start of history); POS (the Structural Positions are synapses and persist); BEAT-R (the model beat is the L beat, and ε is the declared resolution);  
　𝔅\_b (added when tier (b) is used): DOM\[𝒟₁\] (the intervention domain is declared in advance as 𝒟₁) and B\_FD\[𝒟₁\];  
　𝔅\_c (added when there are saturated position–beat pairs): IND\_CLIP, or agreement of the two readings.  
　Part = 0 is handled isomorphically.  
(iii) The only sources of ⊥ are ⊥\_Cov, ⊥\_Open, ⊥\_Ind, ⊥\_Joint, ⊥\_Over and ⊥\_Sat.  
(iv) Subdomain of two-way adjudication: Ω\_dec := {declared instances: exhausted within the budget; no ⊥\_Open, ⊥\_Ind, ⊥\_Joint or ⊥\_Sat among the decisive atoms of Part and HDyn}. On Ω\_dec, with 𝔅₀ ∪ 𝔅\_b ∪ 𝔅\_c declared, Part and HDyn are adjudicated in both directions.  
Proof: (i) by Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2; (ii) by Lemmas 3.1, 3.3, 3.4 and R1 (the event inventory is complete, so the universal negation needs only every event to be 0); (iii) by the value table; (iv) by (i)–(iii) and the completeness of Theorem D1. ∎  
Two-way here means “two-way when B\_FD is declared”; without it, 0 comes only from tier (a) and from a = 0, and tier (b) gives ⊥\_Over.  
Narrowing for 𝒟\_m: exact adjudication on 𝒟\_m requires combinatorial enumeration over the setting beats and has not been completed. Under 𝒟\_m a verdict of 1 uses a 𝒟₁ witness or a pinning witness; a verdict of 0 holds only for the event kinds to which Lemmas C and C′ apply; everything else is ⊥\_Cov. The full working domain of the adjudication theorem is the declared domain 𝒟₁.  
Lemma C (envelope pinning; proved). Let A be the set of settable position–beat pairs, a legitimate setting being one that sets, on any subset of A, the positions to values in \[b\_k, hi\_k\]. The high pin sets every pair in A to hi; the low pin sets to b wherever the current value is above b. If neither pin changes any gate on \[u₀, t\], then no legitimate setting changes any gate on \[u₀, t\], and hence PLUS, MINUS, YIN, YDEC, XDEC, XIN and INJ, the SYN, RELAX and CLIP on positions outside T, and the LEAK and RESET on gating units to which no position of T points are unchanged. Proof: by induction on τ of “the three runs agree on the Structural Positions outside T and on x and y, and on the positions inside T and on v they are ordered as low pin ≤ any setting ≤ high pin”; integration is monotone nondecreasing in v and w (λ ≥ 0, s ≥ 0), the gates of the two pins equal the actual gates, and so do the gates of any setting sandwiched between them; the update of positions inside T, clip(w + P⁺ + P⁻ − ρ(w − b)), is monotone nondecreasing in w (ρ ≤ 1). ∎  
Lemma C′ (reset extension; proved). Under the antecedents of Lemma C, let j be a gating unit to which some position of T points. If, from the last reset through t − 1, no beat reads a position of T that points to j, then LEAK\_j@t is unchanged; if none does through t, RESET\_j@t is also unchanged. Proof: with the gating sequence fixed, v\_j(t) is a linear combination of the value at the last reset and the inputs of the subsequent beats, and positions of T appear only in the terms with s\_i = 1. ∎  
Finite verification: random verification of Theorem D1 on 30 instances and 4002 events within W, of which 700 have FD₁ = 1 (every witness holds on rerun) and 3302 have FD₁ = 0; for the latter, 16986 random samples within 𝒟₁ changed no event (Appendix B.2, first item). Lemmas C and C′: 80 random members, 319 windows, 143 of them with no flip under either pin, 4785 random legitimate multi-beat settings and 401520 comparisons of events of the covered kinds, 0 violations (Appendix B.6).

### 5.4  Validity of the Source Criterion

Lemma CH₀ (same-beat source bearing runs along the actual chain; proved). Let e be an effective event of beat t acting on a Structural Position, and σ′ an admissible removal with removed component set S, and suppose that under σ′ the occurrence or component of e changes. Then S ∩ Orig\_0(e) ≠ ∅, and the change is passed to e along an actual node on Ch\_0 that the removal closes (γ\_j, or the letter edge of x′\_i or s\_i).  
Proof: the pre-beat states are the same. RELAX reads only the w at its own position and is unchanged, so e ∈ {PLUS, MINUS, CLIP}. (1) After removal, vt′\_j − vt\_j = −Σ\_{c∈S} con\_j(c), with con\_j(('in', i)) = w\_ij and con\_j(('I', j)) = I\_j; by the no-opening condition g\_j can only go 1 → 0, and then Σ\_{c∈S} con\_j(c) \> 0, so some c ∈ S has con\_j(c) \> 0, and its SYN or INJ is an actual edge feeding into γ\_j. (2) The occurrence of PLUS\_ij depends on g\_j, and its component is A₊(d\_x x\_i + s\_i): if it changes, then g\_j has closed (by (1)) or s\_i has been removed (via the edge x′\_i). (3) The occurrence of MINUS\_ij depends on s\_i, and its component is −A₋(d\_y y\_j + g\_j): if it changes, then s\_i has been removed or g\_j has closed. (4) CLIP changes only through the sum of its members. ∎  
Lemma CH (across beats, with Trace-Response clamping; proved). Suppose a removal at beat u \< t is admissible under Definition S-3, with removed component set S, and the effective event e of beat t changes in the rerun. Then S ∩ Orig\_aux(e) ≠ ∅.  
Proof: by induction on τ = u, …, t of the stronger statement H(τ): the gates changed in beat τ, the auxiliary positions changed before beat τ + 1, and the events acting on Structural Positions changed in beat τ all have origin sets or carry sets that meet S. τ = u: gates and events as in Lemma CH₀; x\_i changes if and only if s\_i is removed; y\_j changes only through a gate closing; for v\_j, if it was actually reset and is still reset after removal, it is v\_reset in both runs, and otherwise the gate has closed and the carry set after reset is the origin set of γ\_j; if it was actually not reset, the difference is the contribution of the removed components. τ \> u: the letters are the same and the clamping of the Structural Positions is the same, so differences come only from auxiliary positions; a gate going 1 → 0 requires λ\_j(v′\_j − v\_j) \< 0, so λ\_j ≠ 0 and v\_j has changed, and when λ\_j ≠ 0 the carried v\_j feeds into γ\_j; a change in x′ or y′ requires the corresponding d ≠ 0, in which case the carry is kept; RELAX reads the clamped w and is unchanged; PLUS, MINUS and CLIP are as for τ = u. ∎  
The proof uses the coefficient criterion of D15 (λ\_j ≠ 0, d ≠ 0): if “the LEAK component (λ − 1)v ≠ 0” were taken as the proxy for v feeding into the gate, it would overcount when λ = 0 and undercount when λ = 1 or v = 0, and H(τ) would fail. Finite verification: 20098 admissible same-beat removals with a change and 14003 cross-beat ones, 0 violations; chains built by the LEAK-proxy method have 189 violations; of the 78 inadmissible removals with a change, in 47 the removed components are not among the chain origins, which shows that the no-opening condition cannot be dropped (Appendix B.11, Section 5).  
Theorem COR (correspondence between the criterion and the declared source reading; proved). Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g, let χ be a declared instance at the algorithmic level in model mode, and let B be fixed. (1) If e satisfies PS1 and M1₀, then by the declared source reading e is a change with a signal source, with no correspondence hypothesis needed (it still depends on the basic model, record, effectiveness and implementation antecedents listed in 7.2). (2) If e satisfies PS1 and M1\_aux, the same holds under EF1′. (3) If e satisfies PS1 and M2, the same holds under B\_OD.  
Proof: compare item by item with the reading clause. (1) “Under the boundary B declared in advance, an out-of-domain signal”: the removed components are the out-of-domain signals declared in Definition 4, which act from outside B on the medium of B (the entry units and the output-unit membranes), that is, signals in the sense of Section 3 of the General Form; B does not change during adjudication. (2) “Through an actually occurring route of action”: by Lemma CH₀ (or CH) there is c ∈ S ∩ Orig(e), and the change is passed to e along a chain whose nodes all actually occurred and whose edges all carry nonzero functional support; this is not presence, not temporal correlation and not reachability on the slot hypergraph; in model mode the material read-back of the chain is given by Lemma IMP-g (5.5). (3) “Takes part in bringing about an effective change sustained and retained at a Structural Position” and “the proof of source bearing”: e is an effective change on k; when c (together with S) is removed while the state, the laws and B stay the same, the occurrence or strength of e differs, so the action of c is one of the actual conditions under which e occurred as it did—this is exactly the certification by removal comparison that the reading clause permits; the no-opening condition ensures that the change is not transmitted through processes that did not actually occur. (4) The criterion neither tests for nor excludes the joint participation of pre-existing Trace-Responses, auxiliary states and in-boundary mechanisms, and does not require e to be executed by an out-of-boundary carrier; it does not rely on presence, temporal correlation or reachability in a graph; removal is only one of the certification methods, and the criterion also has M2. Hence part (1) of the theorem holds. Part (2): across beats, Trace-Response clamping prevents the dependence from passing through Structural Positions, and Lemma CH gives a chain through auxiliary positions; auxiliary positions are not Structural Positions, but whether they bear Trace-Responses has not been settled upstream, and EF1′ is needed to ensure that the route does not pass through Trace-Responses. Part (3): the bearing interpretation of M2 is exactly B\_OD. ∎  
COR proves only the sufficient direction: Mark^PS ⊆ Mark^A. It yields no conclusion that PS exhausts the declared source reading. Overdetermined cases in which M2 fails are recorded as ⊥\_OD, and whether succeeding actions through Structural Positions have a signal source depends on the open route-termination question (the readings N′ and Ψ of 4.3); none of these is read as absence of a source.  
Read-back of r: under the declared source reading, (r) reads “some effective change in Hist(T) with a signal source falls on the Structural Positions of T, while the other elements of T may be brought about by in-field continuation.” Hence r\_R(T) = ⋁\_{k∈T}\[Mark(k) ≠ ∅\].  
Proposition M-r (∃-type monotonicity; proved). Mark^PS(k) depends only on k and the actual history, not on T; hence for T ⊆ T′, r\_R(T) = 1 ⇒ r\_R(T′) = 1 and r\_R(T′) = 0 ⇒ r\_R(T) = 0. ∎  
Condition P4R (resting medium): the pre-beat state o\_t of the write beat is a fixed point under the rest letter, all components are 0 under rest, and X holds no Trace-Response already left. Condition P4Z (memoryless baseline): v, x and y in o\_t are 0 entry by entry. Proposition Z (proved): when λ, d\_x and d\_y are all less than 1, P4R ⇒ P4Z (under rest the components of LEAK, XDEC and YDEC are (λ − 1)v, (d\_x − 1)x and (d\_y − 1)y, whose coefficients are nonzero while the components are 0); if coefficients equal to 1 are allowed, the class contains counterexamples, and the implication does not hold in general. Lemma Rest (proved): under P4R, with rest continued, the state does not move and all components are 0 (this says only that without field entry nothing is written and nothing moves, not that the auxiliary state is memoryless).  
Theorem SUB (sufficient subdomain; proved). Suppose the pre-beat state of beat t satisfies P4G (no gate opens under the rest letter), e is an effective event of beat t acting on a Structural Position, σ\_t is not the rest letter, and P5ₑ holds (replacing σ\_t by the rest letter changes the occurrence or component of e). Then e is certified by M1₀. P4R ⇒ P4G, and under P4R every effective event satisfies P5ₑ and σ\_t is not the rest letter; hence under P4R every effective write is certified by M1₀, and P4R ∧ P4Z is a special case.  
Proof: the rest letter (none, 0) belongs to the removal domain; P4G gives g′ = 0 ≤ g for removal down to rest; P5ₑ gives the change of e. P4R requires all components under rest to be 0, while YIN has component 1 when a gate opens, so P4G holds; under rest the corresponding component of e is 0 or e does not occur, while e is effective, so P5ₑ holds; if σ\_t were the rest letter, every component of beat t would be 0, contradicting the effectiveness of e. ∎  
P4R and P4Z are proved sufficient subdomains, not necessary conditions for a signal source; P4G ∧ P5ₑ is weaker and allows Trace-Responses already present in X and nonzero auxiliary positions. Finite verification: among random members, 376 effective writes satisfy P4R and 2718 satisfy P4G ∧ P5ₑ, all of them M1₀ (Appendix B.11, Section 5).  
Theorem STR (structure of same-beat certification; nonnegative-weight subclass lo\_ij ≥ 0, including the frozen SNN; proved). Let e be an effective event of beat t acting on a Structural Position. (i) MINUS\_ij: M1₀ holds (remove down to (none, I\_t); w ≥ 0 means no vt increases, so the no-opening condition holds, and MINUS\_ij no longer occurs). (ii) PLUS\_ij with s\_i = 1: under the same removal, either the gate closes or the component changes from A₊(d\_x x\_i + 1) to A₊d\_x x\_i (A₊ \> 0 because e is effective), so M1₀ holds. (iii) PLUS\_ij with s\_i = 0: the component does not change under same-beat removal, and M1₀ holds if and only if some admissible removal closes g\_j. (iv) RELAX: same-beat removal does not change it, and there is no same-beat chain. (v) CLIP\_ij: M1₀ holds if and only if some admissible removal changes the member sum S\_ij. Hence same-beat M1₀ fails in only three places: RELAX, a PLUS with s\_i = 0 whose gate is overdetermined (g\_j stays open under every admissible removal), and a CLIP whose member sum does not change. ∎ For general lo, adjudication is by enumeration.  
Corollary: under nonnegative weights, overdetermination of the gate of a PLUS with s\_i = 0 requires λ\_j v\_j ≥ θ\_j. When θ\_j \> 0 and v was not reset, v′ = vt \< θ, so this can come only from a preset v (initial organization) or from a reset with v\_reset ≥ θ/λ; P4G excludes it. The frozen SNN (θ = 1, v\_reset = 0, initial state v = 0) has no same-beat in-boundary overdetermination on any declared history.  
Treatment of overdetermined cases (a single removal does not change the write, while the signal takes part through an actual route):  
　(A) Overdetermination among signals: two or more signals are each sufficient to open the gate; a single removal does not change the write, but a joint removal does. The removal domain already contains arbitrary subsets of components, so the case is certified by M1₀ and the correspondence is closed.  
　(B) Same-beat overdetermination by in-boundary contributions, where the contribution is itself determined by earlier signals through auxiliary positions: certified by M1\_aux (requires EF1′).  
　(C) Same-beat overdetermination by in-boundary contributions that earlier signals cannot change through auxiliary positions either (such as a preset membrane potential): certified by M2 (requires B\_OD), and recorded as ⊥\_OD when NESS fails. ⊥\_OD means only that the certification methods of this paper are insufficient; it does not give 0. Under the weaker reading “an actual nonzero contribution is participation,” ⊥\_OD would give 1; that reading proves only that a contribution enters the sum, which comes close to what the reading clause says does not suffice for certification; it is not adopted and is listed as open (U1 of Section 10).  
For the readings on overdetermination examples and overdetermination families, see 7.5(4) of Section 7.  
Lemma S⁻ (negative determination; proved under the reading N′). Antecedents: REC, ENT, R1, EF1′. If every effective event in Hist(T) acting on T has no Ch^D15 (or has only dependence through Structural Positions), then r\_R(T) = 0. Proof: by the declared source reading and N′, a change with a signal source must have an actual route of action that does not pass through Structural Positions; R1 ensures there are no off-inventory processes, REC and ENT ensure that the formative history is complete, and EF1′ ensures that the auxiliary positions are not Trace-Responses; hence no change in Hist(T) with a signal source acts on T. ∎ The verdict is written 0\[N′, EF1′\]; without N′ declared, ⊥\_N′.  
Lemma R3σ (field-entry non-interference; proved). Add field-entry nodes to the slot hypergraph. If the read set of the event e is unreachable from the removed components, the removal does not change the occurrence or component of e. Proof as for Lemma R3. ∎ Corollary (identification of continuation): if removing an earlier field entry changes e while e is unreachable on the slot hypergraph with the Structural Position nodes deleted, then the dependence passes only through Trace-Responses already left. In random verification there are 303 such cases, all of them relaxation (Appendix B.11, Section 5).

### 5.5  T\_max Reduction and Read-Back

Theorem TM (proved). Let T ⊆ T′ ⊆ X on the same declared history, with 𝒟₁(T) having A\_T(u) as its settable positions. Then for the Part and HDyn verdicts J: J(T) = 1 ⇒ J(T′) = 1, and J(T′) = 0 ⇒ J(T) = 0 (under the same antecedents). Proof: for a positive determination, the witness for T extends to a setting for T′ (the positions of T′ \\ T take their actual values), which changes the same event with Q and Path\_act unchanged; for a negative determination, in tier (a) unreachability from the enlarged source set still holds, and in tier (b) 𝒟₁(T) ⊆ 𝒟₁(T′); shrinking the target set of HDyn only removes disjuncts. ⊥ does not transfer. ∎ Finite verification: all 15 nonempty subsets of T\_max = {w00, w10, w20, w30} are evaluated exhaustively, with 0 monotonicity violations (Appendix B.2, first item).  
Reduction of Fld: q requires window-end retention at every position of T, so only T ⊆ T\_max need be considered; Part and HDyn are monotone by Theorem TM; r\_R is ∃-type monotone by Proposition M-r; hence Q\_R(T) ≤ Q\_R(T\_max) (in the truth order), and the structure of Lemma 9 of Chapter 3 holds on 𝒦\_g; positions outside T\_max are borne by EF1′.  
Lemma IMP-g (on gating-class program hosts IMP holds by the structure of the model; proved). Let P be the program of a member of 𝒦\_g and L the algorithmic level. For every setting in 𝒟₁ and every removal used by criterion PS (reruns with Trace-Response clamping included): IMP-1, what is set is a variable that the processes actually read, and the change runs along paths on the actual event graph whose first hop is a read edge; IMP-2, the set state differs from the actual pre-beat state only at the changed positions, and a removal differs only in letter components (Trace-Response clamping additionally sets the Structural Positions after u to their actual values); IMP-3, the rerun uses the same transition and the same declared letter sequence and changes no process law, switch or carrier; IMP-4, every changed event lies on read sets forward-reachable on the slot hypergraph from the changed positions (or the removed components), with no passage through a measurement interface or substitute channel. Proof: IMP-2 and IMP-3 follow from the definitions and the determinacy of K; IMP-4 from Lemmas R3, R3σ, CH₀ and CH; IMP-1 from the completeness of the read sets and the actual path required by Lemma 3.2; every set state is a valid program state (K is a total function). ∎ Finite verification: the 78 positive 𝒟₁ witnesses of Instances P and N are checked one by one against the four items, and all hold (Appendix B.9).  
Theorem R7 (read-back for algorithmic-level instances; proved). Let χ be an algorithmic-level declared instance of a member of 𝒦\_g, with verdicts given by Theorem D2 (Part, HDyn), criterion PS and Lemma S⁻ (r), the window-end state, POS and REC. (1) Positive direction: Q\_R(T) = 1 ⇒ T satisfies TraceSet, Part and HDyn on χ, and hence Fld(χ); a positive determination of r given by M1₀ carries no source correspondence hypothesis, one given by M1\_aux carries EF1′, and one given by M2 carries B\_OD. (2) Negative direction: Q\_R(T\_max) = 0 ⇒ ¬Fld(χ), which requires EF1′ and the antecedents consumed by the conjuncts that are 0—when Part or HDyn is 0, 𝔅₀ (with 𝔅\_b, which includes B\_FD, added when tier (b) is used); when r is 0, the antecedents of Lemma S⁻ and the reading N′.  
Proof: check the antecedents of 8.2 item by item against Theorem 5 of Section 10 of the Mathematical Form. Trace-Response: Form by component-wise threshold crossing, Mark by criterion PS and Theorem COR, summation of components by R1, ε the declared resolution. Retention and identity: the window-end state, POS, REC, ENT. Individuation: Definition 5, carriers as declared, agreement of the two readings of clipping. Organizational conditioning and action: Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2, Theorem D2. Source: functional-dependence witnesses and paths on the actual event graph, together with IMP-g. Encoding fidelity: no F encoding is used, and the verdicts are evaluated on R-layer witnesses. The host adjudicated is the program and L is the algorithmic level, so the model is the object, and Dom4 of Chapter 3 has no gap. The positive direction follows from Theorem 5 and Corollary 1 of the General Form; for the negative direction, EF1′, Theorem TM and Proposition M-r give Q\_R(T) ≤ Q\_R(T\_max), and the contrapositive of Theorem 1 of the General Form gives ¬Fld. ∎  
Limits: running the same program on neuromorphic hardware is another instance. Reading back to a material host further requires implementation fidelity (IMP evidence for each kind of intervention: the variable set is one the process actually reads, there is an upper bound on side effects, the process itself is not altered, and there is no bypass; field-entry removal changes neither the state nor the process laws and satisfies the material counterpart of the no-opening condition; the external clamp introduces no unreachable mixed states) and model faithfulness (an item-by-item mapping of the state variables, event inventory, gates, plasticity and saturation, time, signal channels, boundary and out-of-boundary pathways, and parameter domains). If any column is not established, the model verdict stays in column A and is not extrapolated; measurement mode gives only 1 or ⊥.

### 5.6  The In-Boundary Self-Feedback Type

Theorem B1 (one-way criterion under external-clamp LOC; proved). Let e be an event in W with car(e) = B, tgt(e) ∈ T and a(e) = 1. If (i) some external-clamp setting (u, Q) changes the occurrence or the component of e, and (ii) the subgraph of the actual event graph restricted to nodes in B contains a path from Q × {u} to e, then e is a witness of the in-boundary self-feedback type. Proof: under clamping the out-of-boundary nodes are constant sources; induction from the source Q × {u}, as in Lemma R3, shows that changes propagate only through nodes in B; by (i), (ii) and IMP-g, e satisfies the requirement of Section 7 of the General Form that “the material route through which HistCond runs lies entirely within B”. ∎ The converse fails: the absence of a response under the external clamp can result from a clamped mixed state or from two cancelling in-boundary routes, so no 0 is returned.  
Theorem B2 (in open-loop 𝒦\_g the in-boundary self-feedback type and HDyn take the same value event by event; proved). Open-loop declaration: the out-of-boundary letters form a declared exogenous sequence that does not change with the state of the host. Then (1) 𝒞\_B equals the candidate domain of HDyn (the only events acting on T are PLUS, MINUS, RELAX and CLIP, all with carrier B); (2) for each e, the external-clamp reruns and the 𝒟₁ reruns are the same set of runs, so η^cl(e) and η(e) have the same value and the same reason code; (3) the actual paths of positive witnesses lie entirely in B (INJ has an empty read set and cannot be an intermediate node; all state nodes belong to B), so ⊥\_ext does not occur; hence (4) the two sets of verdicts agree event by event (reason codes included), with the same antecedent list (plus the open-loop declaration). ∎ Finite verification: 40 random open-loop members, 80 runs over the two columns; candidate domains identical in 80/80, events in 1252/1252, verdicts in 80/80 (Appendix B.10).  
Theorem B3 (reduction for closed-loop environments; proved). The environment E is an out-of-boundary carrier that reads the output of the host and gives the letter of the next beat; the clamped instance χ^clamp is the open-loop declaration with the same host and the same formative history, its letters being the actually realized sequence. (1) HDyn(χ^clamp, T) = 1 ⇒ in-boundary-self-feedback(χ^cl, T) = 1; (2) every event in W of χ^clamp acting on T has a = 0 or falls in tier (a) ⇒ in-boundary-self-feedback(χ^cl, T) = 0; (3) when tier (b) is used, B\_FD^cl (for the clamped instance) is further required, and without its declaration the verdict is ⊥\_Over; (4) otherwise ⊥. HDyn(χ^cl) is adjudicated separately (it can hold via an out-of-boundary route, as with the witness χ\_e of the General Form). Proof: the 𝒟₁ witnesses of χ^clamp are external-clamp settings of χ^cl, and the actual event graph of χ^clamp is the part inside B of the actual event graph of χ^cl; apply Theorem B1; unreachability follows from Lemma R3. ∎ Closed-loop comparison (Appendix B.9, Section 4): the environment reads output 0 firing at beat 26 and injects into output 0 at beat 27, and PLUS00@27 occurs; when w00, w10 ← b are set at beat 26, the environment does not inject and PLUS00@27 does not occur (the route passes outside the boundary, and HDyn(χ^cl) = 1), while under the external clamp it occurs as usual; on χ^clamp the events acting on T have a = 1 and fall in tier (b), so the in-boundary self-feedback type is 0 (B\_FD^cl declared) / ⊥ (not declared).  
The SNN route “synapse → output neuron → backpropagation to the same synapse”: in Instance P, setting w00 ← b at beat 26 lowers the integration of output 0 below threshold, g0 changes from 1 to 0, and PLUS00@26 does not occur; the actual path (w00, 26) → SYN00@26 → gate g0 → backpropagation → PLUS00@26 writes back to w00, every link having its carrier in B. Cutting the route (switching off backpropagation or plasticity within W) leaves no event in W acting on T, so HDyn = 0, the in-boundary self-feedback type is 0, and Part = 1; this holds by construction from Definition 2(6) and is not offered as an independent finding.  
5.7  Compatibility on the Intersection with Chapter 3  
Subdomains: Ω\_F (the gating never changes and clipping never takes effect); EF4′ (every event in W with FD₁ = 1 directly reads some available T position in its own beat); NS\_act (along the actual history of the host, at every beat and for every T position, the sum of the components truncated by ε is 0; a sufficient condition: every component on T is 0 or has \|c\| \> ε); NN\_act (the F run of the canonical F encoding E\_F is nonnegative on the T positions, Mathematical Form D20). Canonical F encoding E\_F: the depth of a T position is z; potentiation and depression are constant laws taking the actual values; relaxation is a linear law reading its own position; the rest are encoded by their reads. The F recursion is not changed, and the ε truncation is not removed.  
Truncation counterexample: ε = 1/100, T = {w00}, depth z = 3/200, ρ = 1/2, rest, no firing, no other writes, no clipping. The relaxation component −3/400 of the host (\|c\| \< ε) accumulates as usual, and the post-state is 3/400; E\_F truncates this component, and its post-state stays 3/200; from then on the available positions and the window-end retention diverge. The example satisfies Ω\_F, which shows that unchanged gating and inactive clipping do not suffice to guarantee agreement of the trajectories (Appendix B.8, Section 1).  
Lemma NS (proved). On Ω\_F, the actual F run of E\_F agrees with the host beat by beat on the T positions if and only if NS\_act holds. Proof: induction on beats; the difference between the two sides at beat t + 1 is exactly Σ{c\_e : 0 \< \|c\_e\| ≤ ε}. ∎  
The knockouts and local responses of 4.6 of Chapter 3 are one-step operations on the same actual starting state and use no multi-beat F trajectory; so the agreement of trajectories needed on the side of Chapter 3 reduces to agreement of starting states, which Lemma NS gives under NS\_act. On the side of this paper, the multi-beat 𝒟₁ reruns run only on the host and are compared with no F trajectory. Along multi-beat 𝒟₁ reruns, E\_F and the host in general do not agree beat by beat (for ρ \> 0, a setting σ ∈ (b, b + ε/ρ\] puts the magnitude of the relaxation component in (0, ε\]); the adjudication does not rely on such agreement.  
Theorem C4′ (proved). On Ω\_F ∩ EF4′ ∩ NS\_act ∩ NN\_act, in model mode and with RD, for every event in W the η (or κ) of this paper and the verdict of 4.6 of Chapter 3 on E\_F satisfy the following: where Chapter 3 gives 0 or 1, the two are equal (a tier (b) 0 carrying B\_FD\[𝒟₁\]); where Chapter 3 gives ⊥\_Sub, this paper gives 0 or 1 (a refinement in the information order); there is no 1–0 conflict anywhere. By disjunction and the same inventory antecedents, the same holds for Part and HDyn. Proof: by Lemma NS, the states of the T positions on the two sides agree at every beat, and so do available positions, formation and retention; the atoms of Chapter 3 are all evaluated on the same starting states; check event kind by event kind. ∎ Without a declared B\_FD, this paper gives ⊥\_Over at tier (b): there is still no conflict, but no longer equality. Finite verification: 241 random instances with available positions, 73 of them in Ω\_F ∩ EF4′, of which 23 fail NS\_act (including 1 case of a 1–0 conflict on Part); in the 44 cases where all four conditions hold, 3787 atoms show no disagreement (Appendix B.8, Section 2). Compatibility is claimed only on Ω\_F ∩ EF4′ ∩ NS\_act ∩ NN\_act.  
5.8  The Source-Fidelity Condition for D16 of the Mathematical Form  
The F-layer rule of D16 stays unchanged: Mark(i) records the effective changes on i made by pulses with ext = out-of-boundary and by mechanisms whose carrier is outside the boundary; D16 represents source by carrier attribution. The question is under what encoding conditions the F-layer Mark\_F and Src\_F agree with participation-type source.  
Setting: χ is an R-layer declared instance, and E is an F encoding of χ satisfying 8.2(1)–(3) of the Mathematical Form.  
Condition EF-σ(T): every effective change in Hist(T) that acts on T and in which a signal participates is a change made by an out-of-boundary pulse or by a mechanism with out-of-boundary carrier; equivalently, no write by a carrier inside B in which a signal participates acts on T.  
Theorem D16-SF (proved).  
(1) Soundness: Mark\_F(i) ⊆ Mark^A(i), hence Src\_F(T) = 1 ⇒ r^A(T) = 1. Proof: by 8.2(3), the carriers recorded by D16 are faithfully outside B and their action falls on the medium of B, so by Section 3 of the General Form they are signals; the change is the action of the signal itself (Clause 0), and once that out-of-boundary action is removed the change is absent. ∎  
(2) Condition for equality of the complete marks: Mark\_F(i) = Mark^A(i) for all i ∈ T if and only if EF-σ(T). Existence layer: Src\_F(T) = r^A(T) if and only if, whenever r^A(T) = 1, Hist(T) also contains an effective change by an out-of-boundary pulse or an out-of-boundary carrier acting on T. Proof: by (1), the difference between the two is exactly the writes by carriers inside B in Mark^A. ∎ EF-σ is a strong condition, for position-wise equality of the marks; agreement of the two-valued Src requires only the existence-layer condition, and EF-σ is not necessary for it.  
(3) Compatibility with the sources certified in this paper: Mark\_F(i) ⊆ Mark^PS(i) ⊆ Mark^A(i). On true F runs (signals appear only as pulses with ext = out-of-boundary and as mechanisms with out-of-boundary carriers; the occurrence of mechanisms inside B is declared beat by beat and does not change with out-of-boundary input, and they read only the pre-beat values of Structural Positions, Mathematical Form D12), only the Clause 0 branch of the certification methods of this paper can be triggered: same-beat removal changes neither the occurrence nor the components of mechanisms inside B, so Clause 1 and M1₀ are not triggered; F has no auxiliary positions, so M1\_aux does not apply; hence Mark\_F = Mark^PS. Proof: the first inclusion by (1) and Clause 0, the second by Theorem COR, the equality by the preceding sentence. ∎  
This conclusion says only that D16 is compatible with the sources certified in this paper, not that D16 equals the complete Mark^A. Equality of the complete marks takes EF-σ as an independent antecedent, to be declared and verified case by case; it does not follow from true F runs, from reading only pre-beat Structural Positions, or from “no alphabet gating”: whether subsequent writes by mechanisms inside B that occur through reading Structural Positions once acted on by a signal belong to Mark^A depends on the open question of route termination (U2 of Section 10).  
Conditional note (consumes reading N′): if N′ is declared, then on true F runs cross-beat dependence passes only through Structural Positions and does not constitute a route of action of a signal; EF-σ then holds on true F runs and Mark\_F = Mark^A. Every conclusion of this extension carries N′; when N′ is withdrawn, it falls back to the compatibility conclusion of (3). Under reading Ψ, EF-σ fails in the separating example below.  
Separating example (Appendix B.12, Section 1): positions x, y, initial state 0, ε = 0, T = {y}; signal a adds 1 to x; the mechanism copy inside B writes x/2 to y at every beat, with fixed timing and law that do not change with the letter; history (a, rest). At beat 0, x: 0 → 1 and the copy component is 0; at beat 1, copy writes 1/2 to y; with the signal of beat 0 removed, both positions stay 0 throughout and the copy component at beat 1 is 0. D16 gives Mark(y) = ∅, and Mark^PS(y) = ∅ (compatible). Under N′, copy@1 is continuation and r({y}) = 0\[N′, EF1′\]; under Ψ, the chain a@0 → x → copy@1 actually occurs and the removal changes copy@1, so r({y}) = 1\[Ψ\]; when neither is declared, r({y}) = ⊥\_N′. The example shows only the difference between the two readings.  
(4) Counterexamples (EF-σ fails): B5′ (an alphabet-gated constant law inside B in an F model, initial value 0): D16 gives Src\_F = 0, Mark^PS contains the write, and r = 1; frozen SNN Instance P: the carriers of the 36 certified writes acting on T all lie in B (PLUS, MINUS), and the only event with out-of-boundary carrier, INJ, acts on v; so any F encoding that attributes carriers faithfully gives Src\_F(T) = 0, while r = 1 (Appendix B.11, Section 6). The witness χ\* of the General Form (an operator inside B\* executes writes driven by exogenous events, and r holds) is of the same kind under faithful carrier encoding; 6.2 of the Mathematical Form stipulates that F\* is not evaluated directly in F, so no conflict between χ\* and D16 follows from this; the verdict on χ\* agrees with the declared source reading.  
(5) Meaning for read-back: when EF-σ fails or has not been verified, Src\_F = 0 can lose the load-bearing fact “a signal participates in a write inside B”; it then does not satisfy 8.2(6), and by 8.4 it is not read back as r = 0. Under EF-σ, D16 agrees with the complete Mark^A position by position; it is always compatible with Mark^PS; outside EF-σ its 0 is not read back, while its 1 remains sound.  
Finite verification (Appendix B.11, Section 6; B.12, Section 2): 600 random F models whose mechanisms inside B have no alphabet gating: 0 soundness violations, Mark\_F = Mark^PS in 600 cases, of which 79 contain writes inside B that read Structural Positions once acted on by a signal (their complete source is not decided by the program); 600 cases with alphabet-gated writes inside B: 0 soundness violations, Mark\_F = Mark^PS in 359 cases, and in the remaining 241 Mark^PS contains alphabet-gated writes inside B.
